a:T812,All experiments hold the fraction of tokens from repeated content fixed at f=0.1, matching the setup of prior landmark work to enable direct comparison, with 90% of training tokens drawn from deduplicated FineWeb-Edu-Dedup and the remaining 10% drawn from a fixed repeated document pool. Under the fixed-f constraint, repeated pool size is inversely proportional to per-document repeat count R (higher R corresponds to a smaller repeated pool); the combination of pool size and R is termed repetition structure, the core independent variable in experiments. Models are Qwen3-style decoder-only Transformers spanning 34M to 344M parameters, trained across overtraining multipliers of 0.25x, 0.5x, 1x, 2x, and 4x the Chinchilla-optimal token budget, with R swept from 1 (no repeats) up to 20000. A no-repetition Chinchilla scaling law is fit on baseline runs to define Compute-Equivalent Gain (CEG), the ratio of no-repetition compute required to match a repeated run's validation loss to the repeated run's actual compute; Compute-Equivalent Loss (CEL = 1 - CEG) captures the fraction of compute wasted to repetition, with CEG=1 indicating parity with the no-repetition baseline. Closed-form train and test losses are derived for a misspecified linear regression setup with verbatim duplicated samples, to test whether observed damage patterns require LM-specific mechanisms. Three core findings emerge: (1) at fixed repeated token fraction, performance damage varies non-monotonically with R, with moderate repetition of a mid-sized pool causing substantially more damage than either few repeats of a large pool or many repeats of a very small pool; (2) the repeat count at peak damage R_peak follows a power law R_peak ∝ N^{-0.96} with model parameter count N, such that worst-case configurations are predictable directly from model scale; (3) the non-monotonic damage peak is fully reproduced in the linear regression toy model, confirming the pattern arises from the statistical structure of duplicated samples, not LM-specific architecture or optimizer design.b:T46e,Start with the non-monotonic damage curves in Section 4.1 to build intuition for why moderate repetition of a mid-sized pool is most harmful. The power-law fit for R_peak in Section 4.2 is the core actionable result for predicting repetition risk across scales. The CEG/CEL definition and scaling law fitting procedure in Section 4.3 is critical for understanding how raw performance gaps are converted to compute waste estimates. The toy model derivation in Section 4.4 helps avoid misattributing non-monotonic damage to LM-specific mechanisms. Granular optimizer and architecture hyperparameters in the appendix can be skipped on first pass. Pay close attention to the fixed-f experimental design, which cleanly separates repetition structure from total repetition volume to produce reliable, controlled results. Open directions to consider after reading include whether results generalize to near-duplicate and semantically redundant content, and how damage curves behave as f approaches 1, as in full-corpus multi-epoch training; in particular, whether common 2-4 epoch training configurations fall in the high-damage peak region.c:T641,The iterate average optimization problem is formalized as a continuous-time optimal control problem: noisy gradient descent near a quadratic minimum is approximated by the standard Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, with the control objective of minimizing squared error of the returned iterate average subject to a quadratic penalty on control magnitude, forming a solvable linear-quadratic stochastic control problem. The optimal controller applies two corrective forces to the live iterate: pulling it toward the estimated optimum, and pulling it toward consistency with the accumulated trajectory average, to prevent the live weights from drifting too far from the average. Since the exact optimal controller requires unavailable quantities during training (true optimum location, loss Hessian), practical heuristics are applied: the EMA of iterates is used as a proxy for the optimum, reducing the control term to a simple pull of live weights toward their EMA. The resulting PACE algorithm requires only one extra copy of model weights, identical to standard EMA overhead, with no additional complex state. Formal analysis establishes two key results: (1) for general convex losses with unbiased gradients, PACE achieves the standard SGD convergence rate up to a constant factor dependent on EMA strength, meaning it is never substantially worse than SGD; (2) in the quadratic setting used for derivation, PACE strictly reduces the limiting mean squared error of the averaged estimator relative to uncontrolled SGD with EMA, with arbitrarily large improvements possible on specific quadratic instances.d:T5ee,Start with the problem framing to understand the core misalignment between standard optimization targets and the deployed averaged model, the most important conceptual contribution of the work. When reading the optimal control derivation, focus on the intuition behind the control term rather than the full control theory formalism: the core idea is active shaping of the training trajectory to stay close to the average, rather than passive post-hoc computation of an average after training completes. Compare PACE to existing averaging approaches when reviewing the algorithm definition: standard EMA does not affect the training trajectory at all, Schedule-Free computes gradients at the averaged point, while PACE computes gradients at the live iterate and only adds a lightweight pull toward the EMA. When reviewing experiments, pay particular attention to constant learning rate results; robust training without learning rate decay would eliminate a major source of tuning cost in large-scale training. Granular convex convergence proofs can be skimmed for high-level results without step-by-step derivation. Note that current pretraining experiments are limited to small-scale Chinchilla-optimal setups, and validation at larger parameter scales remains open. Extensions to consider include how pull strength should be scheduled across model sizes and training phases, and whether PACE can replace existing EMA plus learning rate decay cooldown recipes in large-scale pretraining to deliver better final models.e:T427,Each dataset defines a gradient update operator U_D(θ)=θ−ηg_D(θ). For two sources A and B, the leading-order difference between U_B(U_A(θ0)) and U_A(U_B(θ0)) is governed by the commutator b_AB = H_B g_A − H_A g_B. Projecting this vector onto the target gradient gives σ_AB(E)=〈g_E, b_AB〉, whose sign predicts which order should give lower target loss. For pairwise planning, the paper evaluates the target gradient at a Trotter reference point θ_ref=θ0−η(g_A+g_B), which approximates the shared first-order drift of both orders and reduces target-gradient drift error. For many-domain scheduling, pairwise preferences define a weighted Lie-Bracket Tournament. The scalable variant deliberately uses a shared θ0 target-gradient reference. With Hessian symmetry, each source needs one Hessian-vector product u_i=H_i g_E(θ0), and Borda or row-sum scores can be computed without materializing the full O(N^2) edge matrix. This changes the estimator relative to the pairwise Trotter version, but makes the ranking procedure practical for large N.f:T9c9,这不是一篇提出新模型并做大规模实验的论文，更接近立场论文加统一视角。技术主线有三步。

第一步，把 attention 解释成 test-time regression。对每个 head，历史 token 经过投影得到 key 和 value，当前 query 要做的是根据过去的 key-value 对，预测与 query 对应的 value。作者强调，这里 attention 不只是“加权平均”，而是在测试时临时构造了一个自监督回归问题：key 是输入视角，value 是标签视角，query 是待预测点。

第二步，区分 nonparametric 与 parametric attention。
- softmax attention 被解释为 Nadaraya-Watson kernel regression 的一种形式。它通过相似度核对历史 value 做局部平均，因此需要保留全部或大量 KV 样本。优点是精确回忆局部上下文，缺点是推理成本和记忆占用随序列增长。
- sparse attention 只能部分缓解成本，但本质仍是保留一部分原始样本、丢弃另一部分。它解决的是“选哪些 token 留下”，不是“如何把经验合并成统计量”。
- parametric attention 则试图用有限维参数或状态，在线学习 key→value 的映射。过去经验不再以原始样本形式存储，而是通过更新规则写入有界记忆。作者把 linear attention、state-space models、fast-weight memories、以及某些 test-time training 方法都放进这个大类。

第三步，提出 lifelong in-context learning 的研究要求。作者认为，真正适合长期流式学习的机制应满足：
1) evictionless merging：不是简单淘汰旧 token，而是把经验合并进状态；
2) bounded memory under fixed hardware：状态维度固定，不随上下文无限增长；
3) online update rule：每到一个新 token，都能低成本更新对 key→value 关系的估计；
4) generalization beyond recall：不仅记住具体观测，还能形成任务相关的充分统计量或规律。

文中对 test-time training 的态度尤其明确。作者认为，在线梯度更新型的 parametric attention 可能是最有前景的一类，因为它允许模型在推理时真正“学”一个映射，而不是只做核回归式检索。但它也带来三个未解决问题：更新效率、容量上限、以及训练目标设计。也就是说，文章不是简单鼓吹线性注意力，而是在给出一个更宽的设计空间：凡是能把上下文转成有界参数化记忆的机制，都值得从 lifelong ICL 角度重新审视。10:T821,This is closer to a position paper than a new-model paper. The technical contribution is a structured viewpoint built in three steps.

First, attention is cast as test-time regression. For each head, past tokens produce keys and values, and the current query asks for the value associated with a new key-like point. In that view, attention is not merely weighted averaging. It constructs a self-supervised regression problem at inference time: keys are inputs, values are labels, and the query is the test point.

Second, the paper separates nonparametric from parametric attention.
- Softmax attention is interpreted as a form of Nadaraya-Watson kernel regression. It estimates the key-to-value map by local averaging over stored examples, which requires retaining the KV cache.
- Sparse attention may cap cost by evicting tokens, but it still operates by keeping some raw examples and discarding others. It addresses selection, not experience merging.
- Parametric attention instead maintains a finite-dimensional regressor or state that is updated online. Experience is absorbed into bounded memory through an update rule rather than stored as an ever-growing example set. The paper groups linear attention, state-space models, fast-weight memories, and some test-time training methods into this family.

Third, it proposes design requirements for lifelong in-context learning: evictionless merging of experience, bounded memory under fixed hardware, efficient online updates, and generalization beyond token recall. The paper is especially positive about online gradient-based mechanisms, i.e. test-time training, because they allow the model to actually learn a key-to-value map during inference rather than only perform kernel-style retrieval. At the same time, it highlights three unresolved issues: update efficiency, memory capacity, and objective design.

So the paper is not arguing for one specific mechanism. It is arguing that any architecture intended for lifelong in-context learning must move from cache-based nonparametric memory toward bounded parametric memory.11:T40b,From the provided full text, the paper appears to be primarily conceptual rather than empirical. The visible sections focus on formalization and taxonomy, not on a new architecture with large-scale language-model experiments. There are no concrete parameter counts, training-token budgets, or benchmark gains in the available content, so it should not be read as a standard empirical paper claiming a measurable improvement on language modeling.

The experimental value, if any, lies in the structural comparison it sets up: softmax attention as nonparametric regression with growing memory, sparse methods as selective retention, and parametric methods as bounded online learners that merge experience. If later sections include toy tasks or synthetic lifelong-learning setups, the key thing to inspect would be whether they demonstrate a real difference between recalling tokens and learning transferable mappings over a stream. Based on the currently visible content, there is not enough numerical evidence to summarize beyond that.12:T4b8,Read this as a research agenda paper, not as a result-driven methods paper. Start with the introduction and identify the actual target of criticism: not transformers in general, but the softmax-attention-plus-unbounded-KV-cache paradigm. Then read the 'Attention as Test-Time Regression' section carefully. That is the conceptual core. The important question is why softmax attention should be viewed as nonparametric regression, and why that representation fails under lifelong constraints.

Next, inspect how the paper organizes the family of parametric mechanisms: linear attention, SSMs, fast weights, and test-time training. The useful distinction is between methods that merely compress or prune memory and methods that truly update a bounded predictor. If the later sections contain open problems or future directions, those are worth reading closely because the paper’s main contribution is the problem decomposition rather than a single technique.

A productive skim should take 30–45 minutes. If you work on long context, online adaptation, agent memory, or continual pretraining, keep three phrases in mind while reading: evictionless merging, bounded representation, and test-time regression.13:T531,1) Define the core metric CoT Token Inflation Ratio (CTIR): the ratio of the average number of reasoning tokens generated by a quantized model versus its full-precision baseline on the same benchmark, used to quantify inflation degree. Calculation strictly splits reasoning tokens (within <think> tags or CoT segments) and answer tokens to avoid metric bias. 2) Strict controlled variable experiments: set temperature=0 to eliminate sampling noise, evaluate weight-only PTQ only (to isolate variables like quantization algorithm and activation quantization), cover dense and MoE reasoning architectures with 4B to 30B parameters, span four reasoning-heavy task categories (mathematics, code generation, scientific QA, agentic tool use), and add light-reasoning general language benchmarks as control groups to verify the task correlation of inflation. 3) Behavioral analysis: quantify semantic repetition rate and number of intermediate steps in reasoning trajectories, distinguish two inflation sources (redundant wording vs repeated reasoning), and verify that extra reasoning steps contribute nothing to accuracy. 4) Mitigation strategy evaluation: test three approaches respectively (prompt regulation for concise reasoning, decoding constraints on maximum reasoning length, QAT) and compare their accuracy-length trade-offs.14:T531,Evaluated models include Qwen3-4B-Thinking, Phi-4-14B (dense), and Qwen3-30B-A3B-Thinking (MoE). Quantization methods cover mainstream weight-only PTQ approaches including GPTQ, AWQ, SmoothQuant, ParoQuant, with bit widths INT4 and INT3. Core results: 1) INT4/INT3 quantization introduces <2% final-answer accuracy loss (nearly lossless) on most reasoning benchmarks, but CTIR reaches 1.15–1.4 (15%–40% increase in reasoning tokens). For example, INT3 quantization on the MoE model reduces AIME25 accuracy by only 0.3% but reaches a CTIR of 1.32. 2) Inflation degree positively correlates with task reasoning intensity: CTIR for light-reasoning general language benchmarks (HellaSwag, MMLU-Pro) is only 1.02–1.07, with negligible inflation. 3) Behavioral findings: quantized models show 8%–15% higher proportion of semantically repeated segments in reasoning trajectories, and the number of intermediate verification steps increases by over 20%, with these extra steps bringing no accuracy improvement. 4) Mitigation performance: Prompting for conciseness only reduces CTIR on a subset of benchmarks, while causing 3%–5% accuracy drop; hard truncation of reasoning length severely hurts accuracy; QAT keeps accuracy loss within 1% while suppressing CTIR to below 1.05, making it the most promising mitigation direction.15:T4d2,Priority reading order: 1) Core experimental result tables in Section 4, especially the comparison of CTIR and accuracy changes across models, quantization bit widths, and tasks — this is the core empirical evidence of the paper. 2) Behavioral analysis in Section 6, to clarify that inflation manifests as repeated reasoning rather than redundant wording, and understand its actual impact on computational cost. 3) CTIR definition and calculation details in Section 5, especially the split logic between reasoning tokens and answer tokens, to avoid metric bias in reproduction or application. 4) Mitigation strategy comparison in Section 7, focus on the internal reason why QAT can simultaneously control precision loss and token inflation. Skip appendix sections on specific quantization algorithm parameter configurations (unless working on quantization engineering deployment). Worth deeper exploration: How numerical precision of reasoning models affects their decision threshold for "terminating reasoning and outputting answers", and whether reasoning length regularization can be introduced during pretraining or RL training to improve model quantization robustness, reducing hidden deployment costs from the training source.16:T714,Dustin is a sparse verification framework specialized for speculative decoding. The problem is formulated as selecting a fixed budget of k KV positions for a verification window [i, i+w-1], with the goal of preserving as much of the original attention mass as possible. To evaluate selection quality, the paper defines Attention Recovery Rate (ARR), which measures how much attention mass is retained by the selected KV subset. The appendix further shows that ARR is strongly negatively correlated with output-logit KL divergence, making it a useful proxy for sparse-forward fidelity. The method has two main components. The first is a hybrid token-selection policy that combines historical attention from the target model with lookahead attention from the draft model. Historical attention is cheap and reflects established dependencies, while draft lookahead provides information about future tokens inside the verification block and helps handle saliency shift. The second is a sparse estimation scheme designed to keep the selector itself cheap. Instead of materializing attention importance across all heads and layers, Dustin searches for a minimal subset of attention heads that is sufficient for accurate importance estimation. This is a crucial systems choice: any dynamic selector on the verification path must be cheaper than simply running dense attention over the full KV cache, otherwise compression is pointless. The paper also positions Dustin against several baselines. Static eviction permanently discards context and therefore struggles under saliency shift. Full dynamic rescoring is accurate but too expensive. Page-level approximations such as Quest avoid exact QK^T but still incur non-trivial scoring overhead and remain slower than Dustin’s sparse estimator in the authors’ analysis.17:T606,Experiments cover the Llama3 and Qwen2.5 families on LongBench and PG-19. The analysis sections mainly use Qwen2.5-32B as the target model and Qwen2.5-0.5B as the draft model, with LongReward as the analysis dataset, whose average context length is about 13.5k tokens. The headline system result is up to 27.85x acceleration for self-attention computation and up to 9.17x decoding-phase speedup on Qwen2.5-72B at 32k context length and batch size 16, with only minimal accuracy loss. One of the most informative observations appears early: at 32k input length and batch size 16, verification can account for as much as 87.5% of total decoding latency. That explains why optimizing verification matters more than squeezing a bit more speed from the draft side. On the method side, the paper shows that historical attention alone is insufficient because speculative verification processes a future block of w tokens, so past saliency does not extrapolate reliably. Draft lookahead alone is also insufficient because the smaller model is imperfect at predicting which tokens the target model will care about. The hybrid policy gets closer to the oracle in ARR, and the ARR-to-logit-KL relationship supports ARR as a meaningful proxy. Another practically important result is negative: a naive dynamic selector that recomputes importance from all heads and layers becomes slower than full-cache self-attention beyond 4k tokens. That finding is broadly relevant for any method that tries to do fine-grained dynamic KV selection during verification.18:T438,A good skim order is: bottleneck diagnosis, metric, system design, then end-to-end results. Start with the introduction to understand why verification, not drafting, dominates latency in long-context speculative decoding, and why both static and standard dynamic KV compression are mismatched to this regime. Then read the observation section comparing historical attention and draft lookahead; this is the empirical basis for the hybrid policy. Next, read the ARR definition and the evidence connecting ARR to logit KL, since ARR is the paper’s main analysis tool. Finally, inspect the main result tables, especially the 32k, batch-16, 72B settings that are closest to realistic deployment pressure. If time is limited to about 20 minutes, focus on the introduction, ARR, the hybrid policy, the head-subset search, the main throughput tables, and the overhead analysis in the section discussing naive dynamic scoring. If evaluating transferability to another serving stack, pay extra attention to the trade-offs across KV budgets, verification window sizes, and model families.19:T411,The system is built around a layered multi-agent framework. Its backbone is an Evolutionary Knowledge Graph (EKG) constructed from 164 mainstream LLM compression methods, encoding algorithmic structure, applicability, hardware constraints, and historical relations as machine-readable memory. On top of this, multiple agents perform hypothesis generation, mutation of prior algorithmic trajectories, algorithmic chain-of-thought construction, and AI peer review for feasibility filtering. Rather than asking an LLM to invent algorithms from scratch, the framework explicitly models evolutionary paths over prior methods, converting blind stochastic search into directed structural mutation. A bandwidth-sensitive Sensitivity Profile provides direct hardware feedback instead of deferred validation. The discovered algorithms are Q-Enhance, an outlier-aware quantization scheme for long-context dense models, and MoE-Salient-AQ, which assigns precision at expert granularity and uses adaptive low-rank error compensation for MoE quantization.1a:T8c4,方法核心是 Typical-Acceptance Invariance Screen, TAIS。它不是提出新的 speculative decoding 算法，而是定义一套行为等价性判定流程。具体有三层：
1) 配对输出比较：同一条 prompt，分别跑 target-only 和 speculative stack，直接比较输出是否 byte-identical。对 T=0 来说，理论上 Leviathan 等人的分布保持性质会退化成逐 token 一致，除去数值非结合性等实现细节；作者把这一点当作实践验证对象，而不是新理论。
2) 统计等价检验：对安全任务的通过率/拒答率差异做 TOST，等价区间设为 ±3 个百分点。也就是说，不要求“完全无差异”，而是要求差异小到落在预先声明的可接受范围内。
3) 效应量约束：逐任务计算 Cohen’s h，并要求最大 |h| < 0.1。这个阈值由扩展实验校准，位置在观测到的 null-consistent 对比之上、传统 trivial-effect floor 之下。

实验设计是五阶段因子化设计。轴包括：接受机制（strict rejection sampling vs probabilistic typical acceptance）、模型对（三组 target-draft 配对，来自 Llama 和 Qwen）、speculation length N∈{1,3,5,8,12}。Phase 1 先跑 5 个独立模型建立基线；Phase 2/3 固定模型对和 prompt，只改变接受机制；Phase 4 扫 speculation length 看是否存在剂量效应；后续扩展再加入更激进的 draft 扰动。

扩展部分是这篇工作的关键。作者没有只测“正常 draft”，而是加入了几种可能引发担忧的条件：
- DPO-adversarial draft：用 flipped Anthropic/hh-rlhf preference 训练出的对抗性 draft，目的是故意扭曲对齐画像；
- GPTQ-4bit draft：测试量化 draft 是否通过 acceptance path 引入偏差；
- 两个随机种子复现；
- fp16 与 bf16 accumulator dtype 切换；
- 70B target + 8B draft 的单臂 scale probe，用来检查生产规模下 refusal 范围是否异常。

从机制上看，这篇工作的隐含判断是：在 greedy decoding 下，只要 target verification 真正逐步约束最终输出，draft 的安全画像不应穿透 acceptance step。作者用对抗 draft 和量化 draft 去压这个假设，结果仍未观察到超出检测阈值的偏差。1b:T820,The main contribution is Typical-Acceptance Invariance Screen (TAIS), a behavioral-equivalence protocol rather than a new decoding algorithm. It has three layers.

First, paired output comparison: for each prompt, run both target-only decoding and the speculative stack, then compare outputs directly, including byte identity. At T=0, the distribution-preservation guarantee from Leviathan et al. should collapse to token-level equality modulo implementation-level numerical issues; the paper treats this as an empirical validation target.

Second, equivalence testing: for safety outcomes such as refusal/pass rates, apply TOST with a predeclared equivalence band of ±3 percentage points. The goal is not to prove exact equality, but to show that any difference is practically negligible within a stated margin.

Third, effect-size control: compute per-task Cohen’s h and require max |h| < 0.1. The threshold is calibrated from the expansion study, placed above the loudest null-consistent contrast and below the conventional trivial-effect floor.

The experimental design is a five-phase factorial setup over three axes: acceptance method (strict rejection sampling vs probabilistic typical acceptance), model pair (three target-draft pairs from Llama and Qwen families), and speculation length N in {1, 3, 5, 8, 12}. Phase 1 establishes standalone baselines for five models. Phases 2 and 3 vary only the acceptance method while holding prompts and model pairs fixed. Phase 4 sweeps speculation length to test dose-response behavior.

The most useful part is the stress testing. The paper does not stop at standard drafts. It adds a DPO-adversarial draft trained on flipped Anthropic/hh-rlhf preferences, a GPTQ-4bit draft, two random seeds, fp16 vs bf16 accumulation, and a 70B target with an 8B draft as a one-arm scale probe. The underlying hypothesis is that under greedy decoding, if target verification is functioning as intended, draft-side alignment differences should not survive the acceptance step. The experiments are designed to pressure-test exactly that claim.1c:T5ab,The confirmatory core contains 16,783 paired samples. The matched expansion adds 44,066 samples, plus a separate 4,006-sample 70B scale probe, for a total near 65k. The paper also mentions a 48,072-sample expansion in one place; that larger number appears to include the unmatched scale probe, so readers should keep the bookkeeping distinction in mind.

Main findings:
- Across matched TAIS probes, every well-conditioned per-task contrast falls within the ±3pp equivalence margin. In the core ledger, 25 of 27 cells pass; the remaining two are not substantive failures but ceiling-region Wald-CI edge cases where p_target = p_spec.
- The largest observed Cohen’s h on matched AdvBench refusal contrasts is only 0.024, far below the 0.1 cutoff.
- Neither the DPO-adversarial draft nor the GPTQ-4bit draft produces detectable safety leakage after target verification.
- The conclusion is stable across two seeds and across fp16/bf16 accumulation.
- The 70B production-scale probe stays within the expected refusal range, but because it lacks a matched 70B target-only arm, it is correctly reported as a scale sanity check rather than part of the main equivalence claim.

The right interpretation is not “perfect identity everywhere,” but “no meaningful divergence detectable above the stated margins and effect-size thresholds.” That is a stronger and more deployment-relevant statement than simply reporting a few refusal-rate comparisons.1d:T76c,The method has two main components: Facet-Probe and ODI.

Facet-Probe is a five-facet audit of ordering effects: option order, evidence-chunk order, document-rank order, image-set order, and mixed-modality order. This is a stronger design than prior work that only studies one ordering axis, because real multimodal systems inherit ordering from many sources: retrieval rank, chunk packing, image arrangement, and interleaving of text and visual evidence. The benchmark spans 12 datasets and four task families, aiming to cover both standard multiple-choice reasoning and multi-evidence aggregation settings.

ODI, or Ordering-Decomposed Item-Response Theory, is the statistical layer that separates different sources of answer flips. The authors argue that observed answer changes across permutations can come from true ordering bias, but also from decoder stochasticity or fragile decision boundaries. To disentangle these, they use a Bayesian 2PL IRT-style decomposition that estimates per-item ordering noise and per-facet systematic bias. A key design choice is the same-ordering control: the exact same input is decoded multiple times to estimate the stochastic floor, and only the excess flip rate under actual reordering is attributed to ordering effects. This control is essential; without it, raw flip rates are hard to interpret.

The paper also includes mechanism analysis and mitigation tests. One recurring mechanism is content rationalization: the model is nudged toward an answer by the ordering, then generates a plausible explanation around whichever evidence appears salient in that arrangement. For mitigation, the authors test training-free prompt modifications, but find that these are modality-conditional. A prompt trick that helps text-heavy settings does not necessarily transfer to visual reasoning or mixed-modality tasks, and different mitigations do not compose cleanly.1e:T664,The empirical study is large: 18 models, including 6 frontier closed-source systems and 12 open-weight models; 12 datasets; and more than 400,000 trials. The model set includes Gemini, Claude, and ChatGPT variants, along with Qwen3.5-VL, InternVL3.5, Kimi-VL, and MedGemma families. Closed models are queried with temperature 0 where possible, and open models are greedily decoded, specifically to reduce the chance of mistaking ordinary decoding randomness for ordering effects.

Several findings stand out. First, none of the audited MLLMs is order-invariant. Screened panel-mean flip rates across facets range from 24% to 50%, and even the best model still flips on 13.4% of trials. Second, stronger models are generally more stable, with a reported Spearman correlation of about -0.95 between capability and flip rate, but capability does not eliminate the issue. Third, self-reported confidence substantially underestimates ordering risk, by 9 to 62 percentage points, so calibration alone is not a sufficient reliability signal. Fourth, the same-ordering control on Gemini shows that even at temperature 0, the excess flips under reordered inputs remain well above the same-input stochastic floor, supporting the claim that the effect is a real systematic bias rather than just decoder noise. Fifth, prompt-level mitigations can help, but only in a modality- and task-specific way.

When reading the results, it is more useful to compare the five facets than to focus only on the overall flip rate, because option-order, document-rank, and image-set sensitivity likely arise from different mechanisms and imply different remedies.1f:T585,Read this as an evaluation-method paper rather than a modeling paper. Start with the introduction and the high-level setup to understand that the target is order invariance under semantically equivalent permutations, not generic prompt sensitivity.

Then go straight to the definition of the five Facet-Probe axes. Each facet corresponds to a realistic source of ordering in deployed systems, and understanding those mappings helps interpret the results.

Next, read the ODI section carefully. This is the most important technical part because it determines whether the reported instability is actually attributable to ordering. Focus on how the paper separates ordering noise, systematic bias, and the decoder stochastic floor. Raw flip rates without this decomposition would be much less convincing.

In the experiments, first scan the main summary tables, then read the capability-versus-flip correlation plots, the same-ordering control results, and the mechanism analysis. If you care about practical fixes, read the mitigation section, but the main lesson is not a ready-made recipe; it is that shallow prompt repairs do not solve the deeper invariance problem.

A 30–40 minute pass should be enough for the main ideas. If you work on multimodal retrieval, long-context fusion, or benchmark design, the appendix details on prompt adapters, dataset screening, and exploratory claims are also worth reading.20:T42a,The authors model an agentic system as a triple of control program P, model interface M, and environment/tool interface E, evaluated under a resource budget C. They define system-level compression: given a shared system on both encoder and decoder sides, the encoder transmits only a short per-instance code that allows the decoder, under identical interfaces and budget, to reconstruct the target object. A stronger system requires fewer residual bits. To make this operational, three coding schemes are combined: arithmetic coding converts conditional model distributions into NLL/codelength; seed coding applies to deterministic or replayable environments by transmitting only a random-seed rank until a valid sample is found; and arithmetic coding is used as a fallback when seed coding fails within budget, ensuring lossless encodability for every sample. A marginal bit value metric is then defined as the average codelength reduction attributable to adding a specific component, enabling unified accounting of tools, retrieval, verifiers, and sampling budget.21:T46b,The method, SafeVec, extracts a refusal direction from a safety-aligned reference model within the same architecture family, then scores a target model by how strongly its hidden states align with that direction under unsafe and jailbreak prompts. The pipeline has five stages:
1. collect last-token residual activations for safe and unsafe prompts at each layer;
2. define the layer-wise refusal direction as the normalized difference between mean unsafe and mean safe activations;
3. select a continuous layer window, avoiding early lexical layers and very late layers that may be dominated by readout effects;
4. use a small set of calibration models with different safety levels to map raw cosine similarities into a 0–100 Refusal Alignment Score (RAS);
5. score the target model using its alignment to the refusal direction on unsafe and jailbreak inputs.

This is a white-box method by design. It requires access to hidden states and benefits from having a family-matched reference model plus calibration models. In return, it avoids generation, avoids judge models, and is much cheaper than output-level safety evaluation.22:T4f5,STEB is a 32.6-hour Chinese-English S2ST benchmark covering six real-world scenarios: drama, audiobooks, advertisements, interviews, news, and commentary. The data pipeline starts from public long-form audio and video. BS-Roformer is used for speech-background separation, Silero VAD detects voiced regions, pyannote performs diarization, and CAM++ speaker embeddings merge same-speaker segments with a cosine similarity threshold of 0.75. Segments are restricted to 3–30 seconds, then filtered by bandwidth, signal-to-noise ratio, and DNSMOS with a threshold of 3.0. Whisper language identification keeps Chinese and English utterances. Each utterance is annotated with transcription, translation, emotion, scenario style, and rich audio descriptions. The benchmark also includes an NV subset with 901 utterances and 1.26 hours of confirmed nonverbal vocalizations. Evaluation covers standard dimensions, including translation fidelity, speaker similarity, and duration alignment, as well as expressive dimensions, including emotion, scenario style, and NV preservation. The expressive evaluation is reference-free: speech is first converted into structured expressive descriptions, then an LLM judge compares source and output with dimension-specific 1–5 rubrics.23:T499,A two-stage recipe.
Stage 1 (action prior): train an encoder–decoder on unconditioned action trajectories only. The encoder compresses an action chunk into a latent embedding intended to capture macro motion semantics over time. The decoder reconstructs the chunk conditioned on the latent, trained with a flow-matching objective to model continuous, potentially multimodal action distributions.
Stage 2 (VLA training): transfer the prior into a VLA policy with three coupled mechanisms: (1) reuse the pretrained decoder as the action output head; (2) early-stage latent distillation—use the encoder’s latent from ground-truth action chunks as a structured target to quickly anchor the VLM-predicted action features, then gradually relax this constraint to allow end-to-end adaptation; (3) history compression—use the encoder to summarize past state-action trajectories into a single latent token injected into the VLM as temporal context.
Key design choice: keep Stage 1 unconditional (no vision/language) to avoid entangling alignment with motion modeling; use flow matching rather than simple regression to better represent uncertainty and multi-modality in actions.24:T756,The method unifies two complementary views. At the objective level, consistency models are treated as forward-divergence-like, trajectory-preserving, and mode-covering, whereas DMD is treated as reverse-divergence-like and directly aligned with the student-generated distribution. At the causal training level, teacher-forcing plays the role of stable offline supervision on teacher trajectories, while self-forcing plays the role of on-policy refinement under autoregressive rollouts.

Causal-rCM combines them as teacher-forcing CM plus self-forcing DMD. In the first part, the student learns few-step causal distillation on offline causal contexts and teacher trajectories, which gives a stable initialization under correct histories. In the second part, the student is optimized on its own rollouts so that the distilled few-step sampler better matches inference-time behavior. The paper argues that this complementarity can be realized either jointly or in a staged forward-to-reverse schedule.

A useful part of the paper is its systematic treatment of initialization for self-forcing DMD. It compares several existing choices, including ODE-pair regression, diffusion-forcing-style causal adaptation, and hybrid TF/DF initialization, against TF-CM initialization. The claimed takeaway is that reverse/on-policy optimization alone is too brittle when coverage is poor, while a forward/offline consistency stage provides a much better basin for subsequent DMD refinement.

The engineering layer is also nontrivial. The authors implement teacher-forcing continuous-time consistency models, including sCM and MeanFlow variants, in an autoregressive causal-mask setting. The custom-mask FlashAttention-2 JVP kernel is the key systems piece that makes this practical. That is likely the most transferable part for researchers working on alternative language-modeling paradigms.25:T49f,From the provided full-text portion, three experimental claims are clear. First, training efficiency: teacher-forcing continuous-time CM converges about 10x faster than discrete-time CM. This is the most concrete quantitative result visible in the available text. Second, the paper performs a systematic study of initialization strategies for self-forcing DMD and argues that TF-CM initialization is more stable and more suitable as a unified recipe. Third, the overall method is presented as a leading open recipe for AR diffusion distillation in streaming video generation and interactive world models.

What is not recoverable from the current excerpt are the full benchmark tables, model sizes, dataset scales, sampling-step configurations, and exact gains on headline metrics. So the paper is better read first for its training decomposition and only then for detailed numbers. When reading the experiments in full, the most important checks are: differences among initialization strategies on rollout stability and long-horizon quality, joint versus two-stage training, and the real wall-clock and memory trade-offs of continuous-time versus discrete-time consistency training.26:T49c,The proposed score, progress advantage, is the log-probability ratio between the RL-trained policy and its reference policy for the same action under the same state: log π_RL(a_t|s_t) - log π_ref(a_t|s_t). The paper formulates multi-turn agent interaction as a stochastic token-level MDP, where the state contains the full history of generated tokens, observed tokens, tool outputs, and environment feedback. Under a reference-regularized RL objective, the optimal policy ratio recovers the optimal advantage function up to the expected scaling from the regularization coefficient. This extends earlier implicit-reward interpretations beyond deterministic reasoning traces, because the conditioning state now includes stochastic environment transitions. The method can be applied at token, action, or trajectory level by aggregating token log-ratios. It is also argued to apply to common RL procedures, including explicitly KL-regularized algorithms such as GRPO and clipping-based algorithms such as DAPO. The design choice is pragmatic: use the actual policy movement produced by RL instead of fitting a separate process reward model that may not transfer across environments.27:T94d,方法部分分成两块：失稳机理分析，以及监督信号设计。

机理分析上，作者把多轮 tool trajectory 写成 action-feedback 序列，关注 RL 训练中 token 分布如何演化。核心观察是，崩塌前往往先出现特定控制 token 的概率尖峰，随后合法工具调用格式迅速下降，错误响应频率陡增。作者进一步说明，这不是能力真正消失，因为在改变提示或放宽格式约束后，模型仍能表现出部分正确的工具使用与推理能力。问题出在 policy mass 被重新分配到少数“结构支点 token”上，生成被这些 token 牵引到退化模式。

在稳定化方案上，作者系统考察了六类 supervisory signals，并按训练组织方式分成 synchronous 和 interleaved 两大类。文中反复强调两者差别：synchronous 是在同一优化阶段把 RL 与监督目标一起混合；interleaved 则是在训练时间轴上交替进行 RL 和 SFT/监督更新。作者发现前者容易出现分布错配，因为监督样本和当前 RL rollout 的状态分布不一致，梯度会互相拉扯；后者更像周期性地把策略拉回到合法结构流形上，因此更稳。

具体监督形式包括：1）SFT 后接 RL，作为强初始化；2）off-policy supervision，用高质量轨迹直接监督当前策略；3）错误轨迹监督，把失败样本也纳入训练；4）hint-based guidance，在生成前提供正确提示或中间线索，但优化时去掉 hint，等于用 hint 只做行为引导、不让模型依赖显式提示；5）process reflection supervision，从训练过程中的中间推理步骤抽取反思文本，总结每一步的优缺点，再与原始错误轨迹配对构造 SFT 数据。这里最有意思的是最后两项，它们不是单纯喂正确答案，而是在结构失稳前给模型更细粒度的过程约束，弥补标量 reward 无法表达“这一轮格式对不对、调用位置对不对”的缺陷。

从与已有方法的区别看，很多 agentic RL 工作把重点放在 reward 设计、轨迹采样或环境搭建上；这篇文章更关心 token-level control dynamics，认为稳定性首先是一个生成结构控制问题，而不是单纯 credit assignment 问题。这一点很像把 RL 训练中的 failure mode 从高层策略失败，下沉到序列建模层面的局部概率异常。28:Taa3,The technical story has two parts: diagnosing the collapse mechanism and designing supervisory interventions.

On the diagnosis side, the paper models multi-turn tool-use trajectories as alternating action-feedback sequences and tracks how token distributions evolve under RL. The key empirical pattern is that collapse is preceded by sharp probability spikes on specific control tokens, followed by a rapid drop in valid tool-invocation structure and a surge in malformed responses. The authors argue that this is not true capability erasure: under altered prompts or relaxed formatting conditions, the model can still display parts of the underlying reasoning and tool-use competence. The issue is that policy mass is being reallocated toward a few structural anchor tokens, pulling generation into degenerate execution modes.

On the intervention side, the paper studies six supervisory configurations, organized into two training paradigms: synchronous and interleaved. This distinction is one of the main practical insights. In synchronous training, RL and supervision are mixed within the same optimization phase. In interleaved training, RL updates and supervised updates alternate over time. The paper argues that synchronous mixing often suffers from distribution mismatch, because supervised trajectories and current RL rollouts come from different state distributions, leading to conflicting gradients. Interleaving instead acts like a periodic projection back onto a manifold of valid structured behavior, which is why it is more stable.

The supervisory forms include: (1) SFT followed by RL as a strong initialization; (2) off-policy supervision using high-quality trajectories; (3) supervision on erroneous trajectories; (4) hint-based guidance, where correct hints are prepended before generation but removed during optimization, so hints guide behavior without becoming a crutch; and (5) Process Reflection Supervision, where intermediate reasoning steps are converted into reflective textual supervision summarizing what each step did well or poorly, then paired with error trajectories to build SFT data. The last two are especially interesting because they do not merely provide correct outputs; they inject process-level constraints that scalar rewards cannot express, such as whether a tool was invoked in the right place or whether the trajectory remained structurally valid.

Relative to prior agentic RL work, which often emphasizes reward design, trajectory synthesis, or environment construction, this paper focuses on token-level control dynamics. Its main claim is that instability is first a sequence-modeling and structural-generation problem, not only a high-level credit-assignment problem.29:T893,The experiments are designed more to characterize failure modes and stabilization trade-offs than to chase a single best benchmark number. The paper reports systematic studies on two model families and compares six supervisory configurations. Even from the provided full-text excerpt, several findings are clear.

First, pure RL can undergo catastrophic collapse. Performance drops abruptly to near zero, valid tool-invocation structures break down, and malformed responses spike. The abruptness matters: this is not smooth degradation but threshold-like instability in training dynamics.

Second, SFT initialization substantially improves early-stage stability. Starting RL from a model already located in a region of valid tool-use behavior reduces the chance of immediate drift. At the same time, the paper notes that SFT alone is not enough, especially under harder OOD settings where generalization can remain limited.

Third, simply mixing supervision with RL in a synchronous way is often insufficient and can even hurt due to distribution mismatch. Interleaved training is consistently more stable and usually yields more reliable final performance. This is one of the strongest practical takeaways: the timing and scheduling of supervision matter as much as its presence.

Fourth, Process Reflection Supervision further improves both stability and final performance. Its advantage is not just more labels, but richer process-level learning signals extracted from failures, allowing the model to learn why a step was wrong rather than only receiving a terminal reward.

Fifth, the paper analyzes learning-rate sensitivity and cross-setting transfer. More stable methods tend to be less sensitive to learning rate and more robust under format-level and content-level OOD evaluation. However, some stronger supervision schemes preserve structure at the cost of OOD generalization, indicating a real tension between structural regularization and open-ended adaptability.

When reading the results, the most informative figures are likely those showing collapse trajectories, control-token probability evolution, and valid-format ratios over training, rather than only aggregate task scores.2a:T5aa,Read it in the order of phenomenon, mechanism, then intervention.

Start with the introduction and the main collapse figures. Do not focus only on task success rate; also track malformed tool calls, error-response frequency, and the probability trajectories of control tokens.

Then move to the analysis section and understand why the authors claim the capability is preserved but hidden by structural failure. If the paper includes prompt-variation or format-relaxation case studies, those are worth close reading because they determine the right remedy. If the issue were capability loss, one would redesign rewards or exploration. If it is structural collapse, one needs behavioral constraints.

Next, study the taxonomy of supervisory signals and the comparison between synchronous and interleaved training. Pay particular attention to what hint-based guidance and Process Reflection Supervision are each fixing. Training-timeline diagrams, if present, are likely useful here.

Finish with the OOD, learning-rate, and transfer analyses. The easy takeaway is “supervision stabilizes RL,” but the useful takeaway is finer-grained: which supervision mainly repairs structure, which harms generalization, and which is more hyperparameter-robust. Around 35–50 minutes should be enough for the main thread; if you want to adapt the ideas to code agents, function calling, or long-chain reasoning, also read the failure cases and appendix details.2b:T44d,Autodata has an inner loop and an outer loop. The inner loop alternates between data creation and data analysis: an agent grounds on source material, generates examples, checks whether they are correct and challenging, measures whether they separate weak and strong models, and feeds the lessons back into the next generation pass. The outer loop meta-optimizes the data scientist agent itself, including its harness, prompts, tool use, and decision policy. The concrete implementation, Agentic Self-Instruct, uses a main orchestrator and four LLM-based subagents: a Challenger that creates examples, a Weak solver expected to fail on many generated cases, a Strong solver expected to solve them, and a Verifier/judge that evaluates both solutions and example quality. For verifiable tasks, the intended criterion is often that the strong solver majority is correct while the weak solver majority is wrong. For less verifiable tasks, the judge scores the gap using generated rubrics. Compared with standard Self-Instruct, the key addition is explicit difficulty control and iterative quality analysis.2c:T445,The proposed method is single-rollout PPO with a Monte Carlo Pass@k critic. For each partial solution, a credit model predicts the probability that at least one of k continuations will end with a correct answer. This predicted Pass@k value is used as the critic for policy optimization. Instead of using GAE with temporal-difference bootstrapping, the critic is trained against Monte Carlo outcome labels tied to eventual success. Token-level advantages are obtained from value changes along the trajectory: a continuation receives positive credit if it increases the estimated chance of future success, and negative credit if it lowers that estimate. A terminal correction term keeps the dense credit signal aligned with the observed final reward. The paper emphasizes Pass@4 as the main operating point. Compared with Pass@1, it gives a more selective signal: easy prefixes are downweighted, while prefixes that remain solvable but uncertain receive more training pressure. As k grows, the value approaches a reachability question: whether any successful continuation exists from the prefix.2d:T5da,The controlled runs use a 4-layer decoder-only Transformer with d_model=256, two attention heads, 2048-token context, an 8192-symbol corpus-specific BPE vocabulary, and 11.5M parameters, or 3.1M excluding embeddings. Each run trains for 4,400 steps with batch size 32 and cosine warmdown over the final 30% of steps. The corpora are TinyStories, where the pronoun-gender rule is densely supported, and a filtered web corpus derived from ClimbMix, where the support is below the measurement floor under the registered counter. Rule support frequency is measured by a frozen procedure, for example she within 16 tokens of a feminine cue; the TinyStories support density for the focal rule is δ_TS=1.67e-3 events per token. Evaluation uses frozen templated probe families with conflict and agree conditions. In conflict prompts, the rule cue and the surface prior disagree, such as a feminine name cue versus a corpus-wide he preference. In agree prompts, they align, serving as a control for whether the construction itself remains solved. Trajectories are classified as recovered, displaced, partial, never, or unstable. A displaced verdict is only counted when the agree condition remains solved. The paper also tracks a contrast margin between the rule prediction and the competing prior. The methodological strength is the combination of behavioral probes, corpus support counters, corpus surgery, and margin analysis, with thresholds, metrics, and directional predictions registered in advance.2e:T5e2,The central trajectory is sharp. By step 925, the model reaches 0.94 accuracy on held-out conflict pronoun-gender probes. By steps 3,500–4,400, it falls close to 0.00 on the same probes, while the agree control remains at 1.00. This indicates rule displacement rather than loss of the underlying construction. The unmodified grid spans two corpora, three data budgets, and three seeds. Support frequency determines the final fate of the rule, while D/N in {1.5, 5, packed} modulates collapse depth without flipping the verdict. Corpus-editing experiments strengthen the claim: flipping rule evidence into counter-evidence at controlled rates, while holding token counts and other corpus statistics fixed as much as possible, destroys the rule with a monotone dose response. A second rule, a/an allomorphy, reproduces the effect on a five-dose ladder inside one corpus, with final accuracy moving monotonically from 0.96 to 0.00 while unrelated capabilities stay at baseline. The reverse intervention is more sobering. Injecting support back into a collapsed corpus at up to 3× the TinyStories support density, producing post-injection rule-to-prior ratios above a surviving TinyStories condition, moves the mechanistic margin but does not yield behavioral recovery that passes the controls. The authors also report the same rise-and-fall pattern in smaller public Pythia checkpoints, with collapse depth following the predicted scale order, and transfer the verdicts to an out-of-distribution probe set.2f:T41a,The study builds a routing-mass atlas and pre-registers six expert families before interventions: math, code, general, Arabic, Chinese, and Spanish. Each family consists of the top 16 experts by lift, excluding always-on shared experts. The intervention is router masking with gate renormalization: masked experts have their router logits set to −∞ before the top-k softmax, and a smoke test verifies zero routing leak. A family is counted as modular only if its on-target degradation exceeds a size-matched random-expert null, estimated as mean plus two standard deviations, and its worst off-target effect is at most one third of the on-target effect. The paper reports both the pre-registered point rule and a conservative bootstrap 95% confidence-interval rule. The same interventions are evaluated with task accuracy, problem-text likelihood, solution likelihood, and per-language likelihood on both FLoRes/Belebele passages and an independent Wikipedia corpus. Qwen3-30B-A3B, with a similar 128/8 MoE shape, serves as a sensitivity control.30:T403,The full study uses under 30 B200-hours and runs on the W4A4 NVFP4 build of Command A+. On Qwen3-30B-A3B, the pipeline recovers a clear reported structure: language-language top-set Jaccard is 0.60, language-task Jaccard is 0.19, for a gap of 0.41. On Command A+, the separation is much weaker: language-language Jaccard is 0.49, capability-language Jaccard is 0.40, for a gap of 0.09. Among the six pre-registered families, only Arabic is robustly modular under the independent Wikipedia run with bootstrap confidence intervals. The other families sit near decision boundaries, and their verdicts change with corpus, metric, or statistical rule. The paper also reports that the high-routing-mass shared core is causally redundant, while high-lift families tend to be entangled rather than selective. Accuracy slices are small, with MATH-500 n=25, HumanEval n=60, and MMLU-Pro n=80, giving binomial standard errors around 0.03–0.10; this is why the likelihood-based and bootstrap analyses carry much of the evidential weight.31:T867,方法由三部分组成。第一部分是 token-wise potential 的定义与估计。给定题目、当前推理前缀和标准答案，在每个 token 位置 t，从该前缀继续 rollout N 次，统计最终命中正确答案的比例，得到 pot_t。文中用 N=64，在整条推理链的每个 token 上都估计，而不是像 Bachmann 等工作那样只抽稀若干点。这样做的代价高，但换来了定位精度。

第二部分是 cliff token 的统计判定。作者不是简单要求 pot_{t-1}-pot_t 超过固定阈值，而是要求它至少超过 0.1，并且通过一侧 two-proportion z-test 的 95% 置信检验。等价地，阈值写成 0.1 + 1.645 * SE_t，其中 SE_t 由相邻两个位置的 rollout 成功率估计方差决定。这样在 pot 接近 0.5、方差较大的区域，判定门槛会更高；在 pot 接近 0 或 1 的区域，门槛较低。作者明确想避免把 rollout 噪声误判成 cliff，这比固定 0.2 或 30% drop 的规则更稳。

第三部分是 cliff taxonomy。作者根据该 token 是否是 greedy choice，以及该位置分布熵的高低，把 cliff 分成 deterministic、uncertain、sampled-off 三类。可以把它理解为三种不同失败机制：deterministic cliff 表示模型高置信地走错；uncertain cliff 表示模型自己也不确定，错误 token 出现在高熵竞争位置；sampled-off cliff 则更像采样偏离，模型的 greedy 也许不会错，但采样选到了坏分支。这个分类不是装饰性的，因为后面的干预和训练结果表明三类 cliff 的可修复性不同。

在干预上，作者做了两种关键验证。其一是删除第一个 cliff token 后重新采样，观察 pass@64 是否恢复；其二是保留 cliff token 但只重采样后续，观察是否仍然无法完全恢复。这个设计直接检验“cliff 是触发点而不是伴随现象”。其后又提出 Cliff-DPO：把 cliff 位置的好坏 token 构造成偏好对，分别在不同 cliff 子集上训练，比较哪类 token 监督真正有用。与一般 process supervision 不同，这里监督信号被压缩到单 token 位置，目标更尖锐。32:T83d,The method has three main components. First, it defines token-wise potential. For a prompt, a partial reasoning prefix, and a ground-truth answer, the model rolls out N continuations from every token position and measures the fraction that end with the correct answer. This gives pot_t at each token t. The paper uses N=64 and computes this densely over the full trace, not just at a sparse subset of positions. The cost is high, but the localization is precise.

Second, it introduces a statistical criterion for cliff detection. A token is not labeled a cliff simply because pot_{t-1} - pot_t exceeds a fixed threshold. Instead, the drop must be at least 0.1 and pass a one-sided two-proportion z-test at 95% confidence. This yields an adaptive threshold of the form 0.1 + 1.645 * SE_t, where SE_t depends on the estimated variance of the rollout success rates at adjacent positions. The point is to avoid false positives in high-variance regions, especially around intermediate potential values.

Third, the paper proposes a taxonomy of cliff tokens: deterministic, uncertain, and sampled-off. The categorization uses whether the token is the greedy choice and the entropy of the local token distribution. Intuitively, deterministic cliffs correspond to confident but wrong decisions; uncertain cliffs arise at ambiguous, high-entropy positions; sampled-off cliffs reflect failures introduced by sampling away from a better-ranked continuation. This taxonomy is not cosmetic. It becomes central in the intervention and training results.

The causal validation is well designed. The authors delete the first cliff token and resample from that point, then compare this with keeping the cliff token fixed and only resampling the suffix. If recovery happens only when the cliff token is removed, that supports the claim that the token is a trigger rather than a post hoc marker. They then build Cliff-DPO by constructing preference pairs around cliff positions and training on different cliff subsets separately. Compared with generic process supervision, this is a much sharper token-level supervision signal.33:T6fa,The study covers seven models and three math reasoning benchmarks: Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-0.6B, Llama-3.1-8B, Llama-3.2-3B, Llama-3.2-1B, and Gemma-3-4B, evaluated on GSM1K, MATH500, and AIME 2025. Token-wise potential is estimated with 64 rollouts per position.

The most important result is the causal intervention. Deleting the first cliff token and resampling restores pass@64 to 1.0, while keeping that token and only resampling the continuation fails to fully recover. That is the strongest evidence in the paper: cliff tokens behave like actual failure triggers, not merely locations where failure becomes visible.

A second result is the cross-model taxonomy analysis. Deterministic cliffs appear relatively scale-invariant, whereas uncertain and sampled-off cliffs better reflect model-specific gaps or scale asymmetries. In practical terms, some errors persist even as models scale because they are confident misgeneralizations, while others are more about uncertainty or decoding-induced branch selection.

A third result comes from Cliff-DPO. Training on uncertain and sampled-off cliff subsets improves reasoning performance, while training on deterministic cliffs does not. The full excerpt does not include every hyperparameter or table entry, but the qualitative conclusion is already informative: token-level supervision helps when the failure is near a ranking boundary or caused by sampling, and helps much less when the model is confidently wrong.

The paper also reports that the adaptive threshold varies roughly from 0.18 to 0.24 depending on the local potential regime, with stricter thresholds in higher-variance middle regions. This is a small but important implementation detail because it explains why the method is more robust than fixed-drop heuristics.34:T500,Read it in the following order: definition, causal validation, taxonomy, then Cliff-DPO.
1. Start with the formalization section. Focus on token-wise potential, dense rollout estimation with N=64, and the adaptive z-test threshold.
2. Look closely at the example figures showing potential collapse along a trace. They build the right intuition quickly.
3. Spend most of the time on the intervention experiment in Section 3.2. The delete-and-resample versus keep-and-resample comparison is the core of the paper.
4. Then read the taxonomy sections, especially how deterministic, uncertain, and sampled-off cliffs are defined using greedy choice and entropy.
5. Finish with Cliff-DPO. The most interesting question is not the training recipe itself, but why deterministic cliffs are resistant to this kind of supervision while the other two categories are not.

If time is limited, the formal definition, the main causal experiment, and the per-category Cliff-DPO results are enough to capture the paper’s contribution in about 30–40 minutes. For deeper reading, it is worth thinking about two extensions: how sensitive token-wise potential is to the rollout decoding policy, and whether the same framework transfers beyond math reasoning to code or multimodal reasoning traces.35:T6bd,The method has three pieces. First, the paper constructs behavior directions in two ways: data-driven difference-in-means directions from activations, and hand-picked directions from lm_head rows associated with behavior-specific tokens. Detection is built both ways; intervention, especially refusal, is mainly derived from lm_head token directions. Second, the authors do causal testing rather than stopping at probing. They inject directions into the residual stream with forward hooks and measure behavioral changes layer by layer at inference time. Third, they analyze the geometry and the mechanism behind the mismatch. For formatting, detection and control collapse onto essentially the same axis. For hallucination, the cosine between detection and intervention is only about 0.12, roughly an 83-degree separation. They also rule out a construction artifact: even a detector built purely from activations, without hand-chosen tokens, remains misaligned with control, with cosine around -0.06. A key conceptual point appears later in the paper: cosine is not a steerability oracle. Detection is not a single privileged vector but a high-dimensional equivalence class, while steerability is functional. What matters is whether the control direction also serves as a detector, not whether one static angle is large. Mechanistically, the paper traces the hallucination mismatch to the output mapping, where an entity-copying pathway dominates the epistemic signal. As a result, pushing along the detection direction can strengthen name completion and make fabrication worse instead of triggering refusal. The authors then try a partial fix by rotating the detection direction 15 degrees toward the intervention direction.36:T5dd,The main experiments are run on Gemma 2-2B-it, a 26-layer model with 2304-dimensional residual stream, using inference-time hooks only and no fine-tuning. Two behaviors are contrasted. For output format, detection and control are effectively aligned, which supports the single-axis story for some shallow behaviors. For hallucination, real vs fake entities become perfectly linearly separable from layer 5 onward, with AUC = 1.000, yet the cosine between the detection direction and the refusal intervention direction is only about 0.12. A second detector built directly from activations, without hand-picked tokens, gives cosine = -0.06 against the intervention direction, reinforcing that the gap is not an artifact of direction construction. The cross-model study includes Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct, Qwen-2.5-1.5B-Instruct, Gemma 2-9B-it, and Gemma 2-2B base/instruct, spanning 1B to 9B parameters. The cosine stays in the narrow range [0.12, 0.20]. The base vs instruct comparison on Gemma 2-2B is especially informative because the values are nearly identical, pointing to a pretraining origin. In the repair experiment, rotating 15 degrees from the detection direction toward the intervention direction raises refusal rates to 73% and 60% on two held-out entity categories, with only 1.8% false positives on N=115 examples. 52%。这个差异支持一个较窄但重要的结论：影响是议题选择性的，并非所有与页面或公司相关的内容都会同等受 PAW 编辑影响。实验设计的强点是同页配对，减少了页面热度、主题域和文本质量带来的混杂。需要注意，提供内容中没有完整展示 counterfactual training influence 和 fine-tuning ablation This raises a fundamental question: given that we will return an iterate average, how should we change training to improve the performance of this average? We study this question by formulating optimizer design for the iterate-average estimator as an optimal-control problem. In a continuous-time stochastic quadratic model, we so","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-23T18:47:40Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2606_25086.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","stat.ML"],"summary":{"core_problem":"当前几乎所有LM训练流水线（预训练与微调均包含）最终部署的模型都不是训练的最终迭代权重，而是训练迭代的指数移动平均（EMA）或其他形式的迭代平均结果，用于稳定优化、提升最终性能。但现有优化器（如AdamW）与学习率调度的设计目标是最小化最终迭代点的损失，并非优化实际部署的平均模型的性能。此前仅有经验观察显示使用EMA后可采用更大学习率、更弱的学习率衰减，尚未有针对迭代平均目标专门设计的优化算法，也缺乏对应的收敛性保证。","importance":"优化器的优化目标（最终迭代点）与实际部署模型（迭代平均）之间的错位是大模型训练中普遍存在但长期未被针对性解决的问题。研究推导出的PACE优化器是标准AdamW的轻量封装，内存开销与普通EMA相当，对现有训练流水线改动极小，且具备形式化的收敛保证。该方法可在不使用学习率衰减的情况下达到甚至超过经过精细调参的AdamW+EMA的性能，若能在前沿大模型规模上复现效果，可大幅降低调参成本，提升微调与预训练的稳定性。","technical_approach":"研究将迭代平均优化问题形式化为连续时间下的最优控制问题：在二次目标的随机梯度下降场景下（极小值附近的噪声梯度下降可通过Ornstein-Uhlenbeck过程近似），目标是最小化返回的迭代平均的平方误差，同时对控制信号的幅值施加二次惩罚，构成可求解的线性二次随机控制问题。最优控制解的直觉是：在常规梯度更新之外，对当前迭代权重施加两个方向的拉力，一是拉向最优点的估计值，二是拉向与历史轨迹平均一致的方向，避免当前权重偏离平均轨迹过远。由于精确最优控制需要用到训练时无法获取的最优点位置、损失海森矩阵等信息，研究通过一系列启发式近似简化：用迭代的EMA作为最优点的估计，将控制项简化为当前权重向EMA权重的拉力，最终得到PACE算法。PACE仅需要额外存储一份EMA权重（与普通EMA的内存开销一致），无其他复杂状态。理论分析证明两点：一是对于一般凸损失、无偏随机梯度场景，PACE返回的迭代平均估计收敛率达到标准SGD的收敛率，仅相差一个与EMA强度相关的常数因子，不会显著差于SGD；二是在推导算法的二次场景下，PACE的极限均方误差严格小于无控制的SGD+EMA，在特定二次问题上误差降低幅度可以任意大。","experiments":"在1-2B参数规模的微调实验中（覆盖SmolLM2-1.7B、Qwen3-1.7B、Gemma3-1B三个模型，在smol-smoltalk数据集上验证），采用恒定学习率（无cosine或WSD衰减）的PACE，其验证交叉熵均严格优于两个基线：经过最优学习率和调度调优的AdamW，以及AdamW训练后计算的EMA结果，且三个随机种子的实验结果稳定。在Chinchilla最优计算预算的小规模预训练实验中，PACE性能与同样基于迭代平均、无需学习率衰减的Schedule-Free算法相当，且与学习率衰减到零的AdamW性能持平或更优，证明无需显式学习率调度即可实现稳定训练。","reading_guide":"首先关注问题提出部分，理解“优化目标是最终迭代点、部署模型是迭代平均”这一核心错位，这是整个工作最关键的概念出发点。阅读最优控制推导部分时，重点把握控制项的直觉含义——主动约束训练轨迹向平均靠拢，而非训练结束后被动计算平均，无需深入控制论的形式化细节。阅读算法定义部分时，注意区分PACE与现有平均类方法的差异：普通EMA完全不影响训练轨迹，仅在事后计算平均；Schedule-Free在平均点上计算梯度进行更新；PACE则保持在迭代点上计算梯度，仅增加一个指向EMA的轻量拉力项。阅读实验部分时，重点关注恒定学习率下的稳定性结果——若无需学习率衰减即可达到最优性能，可大幅减少大规模训练中的调度调参成本。凸优化收敛证明部分可仅关注核心结论，无需逐行推导。注意目前的预训练实验仅在小规模Chinchilla最优设置下完成，更大参数规模下的效果仍待验证。可延伸思考：拉力项强度超参数应如何随模型规模、训练阶段动态调整；该方法是否可替代大规模预训练冷却阶段现有的EMA+学习率衰减组合，进一步提升最终模型性能。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Nearly all production LM training pipelines, for both pretraining and fine-tuning, return an iterate average (most commonly an exponential moving average of training weights) rather than the final training iterate, as averaging improves optimization stability and final performance. Existing optimizers such as AdamW and associated learning rate schedules are designed to minimize loss at the final iterate, not at the averaged model that is actually deployed. Prior work has only produced empirical observations that averaging enables higher learning rates and reduced decay; no optimizer has been explicitly derived for the iterate average objective, with accompanying convergence guarantees.","importance":"The misalignment between optimizer target (final iterate) and deployed model (iterate average) is a ubiquitous but largely unaddressed gap in LM training. The PACE optimizer derived for this objective is a lightweight wrapper around standard AdamW with memory overhead identical to standard EMA, requiring minimal changes to existing training pipelines and supported by formal convergence guarantees. The method matches or exceeds performance of carefully tuned AdamW with EMA even when using a constant learning rate with no decay; if results scale to frontier model sizes, it can substantially reduce tuning overhead and improve stability across fine-tuning and pretraining workloads.","technical_approach":"$c","experiments":"On fine-tuning workloads across three 1-2B parameter models (SmolLM2-1.7B, Qwen3-1.7B, Gemma3-1B) evaluated on smol-smoltalk, PACE with a constant learning rate (no cosine or WSD decay) achieves strictly lower validation cross-entropy than both baselines: well-tuned AdamW with the best available learning rate schedule, and post-hoc EMA computed from AdamW iterates, with consistent results across three random seeds. On small-scale Chinchilla-optimal pretraining runs, PACE performs competitively with Schedule-Free, another averaging-based optimizer that removes the need for learning rate decay, and matches or outperforms AdamW with a fully decayed learning rate, demonstrating stable training without explicit scheduling.","reading_guide":"$d","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇会改你的 optimizer prior：既然产出的是 EMA，就别再只优化 live weights；建议立刻在内部 AdamW+EMA recipe 上做小规模复现。","en":"This should update your optimizer prior: if you ship an EMA, stop optimizing only the live weights; worth reproducing on your AdamW+EMA recipe immediately."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Kwok Chun Au","Adam Block"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["optimizer","EMA","iterate-averaging","AdamW","training-dynamics"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25086","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.25086","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.24993","title":"The Geometry of Sequential Learning: Lie-Bracket Prediction of Transfer Order","abstract":"Sequential learning is order-dependent: from Pile-style next-token domain adaptation to instruction-SFT and DPO, N candidate sources induce N! possible curricula. We show that the local order effect is governed by a computable geometric quantity, the Lie-bracket commutator of gradient update fields, yielding a pairwise score for whether A->B or B->A is better for a target domain. The pairwise bracket primitive also defines a Lie-Bracket Tournament: with a shared theta_0 target-gradient reference","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-06-23T15:39:38Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2606_24993.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"论文解决的是顺序学习中的课程排序问题：给定多个源域或训练阶段，如何在不枚举 N! 个顺序的情况下，预测哪个训练顺序会让目标域损失更低。这个问题覆盖 Pile 风格的领域继续预训练、instruction SFT、DPO、diffusion adaptation 等场景。核心难点是，数据源对应的梯度更新算子通常不可交换，A 后 B 与 B 后 A 会把参数推到不同位置，目标域表现也会不同。","importance":"这篇工作的价值在于把“训练顺序”从经验搜索问题转成一个可计算的局部几何量。它对预训练和 post-training 都有直接启发：数据配比之外，数据顺序也可以用梯度、Hessian-vector product 和目标域梯度来做低成本规划。贡献量级主要在方法论层面：它不提供新的模型结构或数据集，而是给出一个可部署的 curriculum planner。需要注意的是，它依赖局部近似、目标梯度估计和训练步长条件；长 horizon 下准确率下降，说明它更适合作为排序先验，而不是完整替代真实训练验证。","technical_approach":"技术核心是把两个数据源 A、B 的梯度更新看作两个非线性向量场。单步更新为 U_D(θ)=θ−ηg_D(θ)，两个顺序 U_B(U_A(θ0)) 与 U_A(U_B(θ0)) 的 leading-order 差异由 Lie bracket 控制：b_AB = H_B g_A − H_A g_B。再把这个位移投影到目标域梯度 g_E 上，得到 σ_AB(E)=〈g_E, b_AB〉，其符号用于判断 A→B 和 B→A 哪个更可能降低目标域损失。论文还引入 drift-matched Trotter reference：θ_ref=θ0−η(g_A+g_B)，用于在共享一阶漂移附近估计目标梯度，减少只在 θ0 取 g_E 带来的漂移误差。多数据源排序部分把 pairwise bracket 变成 weighted tournament。可扩展版本为了降低复杂度，改用共享 θ0 目标梯度引用；利用 Hessian 对称性，每个源域只需一次 HVP：u_i=H_i g_E(θ0)。Borda/row-sum 排序不需要显式构造 O(N^2) 边矩阵，可由源域梯度和 u_i 聚合后线性计算，再做 O(N log N) 排序。这个设计牺牲了 pair-specific Trotter reference 的局部精度，换来大 N 场景下的可用性。","experiments":"实验覆盖多个训练范式。instruction SFT 和 DPO 中，k=1 的 pairwise 顺序预测符号准确率分别达到 98.1% 和 98.9%；当训练 horizon 增加到 k=20，顺序决策准确率仍高于 72%。在小规模全排列验证中，方法找回 3! 个 schedules 中最优顺序的比例为 87.5%，把最优顺序排进 top-2 的比例为 96.0%。在大 N 排序中，它对 85 个 Stack 编程语言源域、目标为 Python 的设置达到 sampled percentile 的 99th；在 56 个 MMLU subjects 上达到 99.0–99.6th sampled percentile。长 horizon 与 wall-clock 实验显示，1,020 次 Llama trials 中，k=20 的准确率为 81.5%，k=50 降到 65.3%；规划成本在 k≈3 左右开始低于直接尝试两种顺序。论文还保留了 Pile-style 继续预训练领域适配和 diffusion adaptation 实验，用来说明同一 bracket primitive 不限于 next-token LM。","reading_guide":"精读重点有四处。第一，先看 Lie bracket 推导，尤其是 θ_AB 与 θ_BA 的二阶差异如何落到 H_B g_A − H_A g_B；这里决定了方法的适用边界。第二，看 drift-matched Trotter estimator 与 shared-reference tournament 的区别：前者更适合 pairwise 决策，后者服务于大 N 排序。第三，看 Borda/row-sum 的可扩展推导，特别是为什么 Hessian 对称性能把 O(N^2) pairwise scoring 压到每个源域一次 HVP。第四，看 long-horizon 结果和失败趋势。k=1 的高准确率说明局部几何信号很强，k=50 的下降说明真实训练中的非线性、目标梯度漂移和优化器状态会逐步削弱局部预测。值得深挖的是：该方法在 Adam、momentum、LoRA 子空间、MoE expert routing、数据重复和 annealing 阶段下是否仍保持同样的 bracket 解释。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses curriculum ordering in sequential learning. Given multiple source domains or post-training stages, the goal is to predict which order will minimize loss on a target distribution without evaluating N! possible curricula. The problem appears in domain-adaptive pretraining, instruction tuning, DPO, and diffusion adaptation. The key observation is that gradient update operators are generally non-commutative, so training on A then B and B then A can lead to different parameter states and different target losses.","importance":"The main contribution is a computable primitive for data-order planning. Instead of treating curriculum order as an empirical search problem, the paper links local order effects to a Lie-bracket term derived from gradients and Hessian-vector products. This is directly relevant to pretraining and post-training pipelines where many data sources are mixed or staged. The result should be read as a planning prior rather than a complete replacement for validation runs: it is local, depends on target-gradient estimation, and degrades over longer horizons. Still, it gives a concrete way to reason about ordering beyond heuristics such as domain similarity or dataset quality scores.","technical_approach":"$e","experiments":"The empirical section tests the primitive across several regimes. In instruction SFT and DPO, pairwise sign accuracy at k=1 reaches 98.1% and 98.9%, respectively. At k=20, order-decision accuracy remains above 72% in both settings. For full enumeration over three-source schedules, the method recovers the best of all 3! orders 87.5% of the time and places the best order in the top two 96.0% of the time. In larger scheduling problems, it ranks 85 Stack programming-language sources for a Python target in the 99th sampled percentile, and reaches the 99.0–99.6th sampled percentile across 56 MMLU subjects. Long-horizon Llama experiments over 1,020 trials show 81.5% accuracy at k=20 and 65.3% at k=50. The wall-clock study reports that planning becomes cheaper than trying both orders around k≈3. The paper also includes Pile-style pretraining-domain adaptation and diffusion adaptation as cross-model checks.","reading_guide":"The most important part to read carefully is the derivation of the local commutator term, since it explains both the strength and the limits of the method. Then compare the pairwise Trotter estimator with the shared-reference tournament estimator; the former is designed for local pair decisions, while the latter is designed for scalable ranking. The row-sum derivation is worth checking because it is the step that turns pairwise ordering into an O(N) HVP procedure followed by sorting. The long-horizon experiments are also important: they show that the signal remains useful beyond one-step predictions, but also that local geometry loses accuracy as training trajectories move farther from the reference point. Follow-up questions include how the bracket behaves with Adam states, LoRA-only updates, MoE routing changes, data repetition, and cooldown or annealing phases.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"用李括号量化训练顺序效应的思路极具实操性，应立刻在内部多域预训练/多阶段SFT的ablation中复现验证。","en":"The Lie-bracket based training order effect quantification is highly practical; immediately replicate in internal multi-domain pretrain / multi-stage SFT ablations."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["John Sweeney"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["curriculum_learning","sequential_learning","lie_bracket","training_recipe","llm_pretraining"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24993","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.24993","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2606.24901","title":"LLM Evolution as an Industry-Scale Ecosystem: A Lifecycle Perspective on Continual Learning","abstract":"Continual learning capability is critical for Industrial LLMs, as deployed models must be continuously updated to meet evolving requirements and environments, rather than repeatedly retrained from scratch.
